[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 25.0 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"f29320","wall_time_seconds":0.9345761,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1601,"output":"// The Signal Observatory draws the relationship hiding in a statement as a\\r\\n// figure between the statement\'s own words.\\r\\n//\\r\\n// This is the most machine-readable bank in the Centre, and the world leans on\\r\\n// that rather than on judgment. Measured across its 48 items:\\r\\n//\\r\\n// - All 48 credited answers parse as `type, \\"indicator\\"` groups, including the\\r\\n//   multi-relationship form (`conditional, \\"Only if\\"; causal, \\"because\\" and\\r\\n//   \\"accelerates\\"`).\\r\\n// - All 109 indicator words anchor to real words in their own statement, so\\r\\n//   every figure is drawn from text the student can see rather than from a\\r\\n//   position I chose. Three shapes of indicator needed handling to get there:\\r\\n//   a quote carrying a trailing period (\\"lags.\\"), a split comparative\\r\\n//   (\\"more … than\\"), and a separated pair (\\"stronger than\\" appearing as\\r\\n//   \\"stronger ... than\\").\\r\\n// - The authored relationship count agrees with the parsed group count on all\\r\\n//   48. Item 40 once declared four and listed three; the content was fixed, and\\r\\n//   the parse still follows the credited answer, because that is what the\\r\\n//   student is graded against.\\r\\n//\\r\\n// What this module does NOT do is decide which words are jargon. The Rung 4\\r\\n// dimming is still an open question on the drill\'s README and would need a\\r\\n// technical-vocabulary list nobody has written; dimming words by my own guess\\r\\n// at what looks technical would show a student my judgment as his teaching.\\r\\n\\r\\nimport type { ActivityItem } from \\"../../api/types\\";\\r\\n\\r\\nexport type RelationKind = \\"conditional\\" | \\"causal\\" | \\"comparative\\";\\r\\n\\r\\nexport interface Relation {\\r\\n  kind: RelationKind;\\r\\n  /** The indicator words as the bank quotes them. */\\r\\n  indicators: string[];\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * Where each indicator sits in the statement, as [start, end) offsets. A\\r\\n   * figure is drawn between these, so an indicator that cannot be found is\\r\\n   * never given a position.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  anchors: { text: string; start: number; end: number }[];\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport interface Signal {\\r\\n  order: number;\\r\\n  rung: number;\\r\\n  statement: string;\\r\\n  relations: Relation[];\\r\\n  /** The count the bank declares in its own explanation, when it declares one. */\\r\\n  declared: number | null;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nconst GROUP =\\r\\n  /(conditional|causal|comparative|comparison)\\\\s*,\\\\s*((?:\\"[^\\"]+\\"(?:\\\\s+and\\\\s+)?)+)/gi;\\r\\nconst QUOTED = /\\"([^\\"]+)\\"/g;\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Words too common to anchor a figure to on their own. */\\r\\nconst UNANCHORABLE = new Set([\\"to\\", \\"the\\", \\"a\\", \\"an\\", \\"of\\", \\"in\\", \\"on\\"]);\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction normalizeKind(raw: string): RelationKind {\\r\\n  const k = raw.toLowerCase();\\r\\n  // The bank writes \\"comparative\\"; the drill\'s badge says \\"Comparison\\". One\\r\\n  // name internally so the geometries table cannot drift from the parser.\\r\\n  return k === \\"comparison\\" ? \\"comparative\\" : (k as RelationKind);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction escape(text: string): string {\\r\\n  return text.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\\\\]\\\\\\\\]/g, \\"\\\\\\\\$&\\");\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction locate(word: string, statement: string): { text: string; start: number; end: number } | null {\\r\\n  const m = statement.match(new RegExp(`\\\\\\\\b${escape(word)}\\\\\\\\b`, \\"i\\"));\\r\\n  if (!m || m.index === undefined) return null;\\r\\n  return { text: statement.slice(m.index, m.index + m[0].length), start: m.index, end: m.index + m[0].length };\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Where an indicator sits in its statement.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Tried whole first, because \\"only if\\" is one indicator and should anchor as\\r\\n * one. When the whole phrase is not contiguous in the text, it is split: a\\r\\n * comparative like \\"more … than\\" is authored as a pair precisely because the\\r\\n * statement separates them, and the figure needs both ends.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport function anchorsFor(indicator: string, statement: string) {\\r\\n  const whole = indicator.trim().replace(/[.,]+$/, \\"\\").trim();\\r\\n  const direct = locate(whole, statement);\\r\\n  if (direct) return [direct];\\r\\n\\r\\n  const out: { text: string; start: number; end: number }[] = [];\\r\\n  for (const part of whole.split(/…|\\\\.\\\\.\\\\./).map((p) => p.trim()).filter(Boolean)) {\\r\\n    const asPhrase = locate(part, statement);\\r\\n    if (asPhrase) {\\r\\n      out.push(asPhrase);\\r\\n      continue;\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    for (const raw of part.split(/\\\\s+/)) {\\r\\n      const word = raw.replace(/[.,]+$/, \\"\\");\\r\\n      if (!word || UNANCHORABLE.has(word.toLowerCase())) continue;\\r\\n      const at = locate(word, statement);\\r\\n      if (at) out.push(at);\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return out.sort((a, b) => a.start - b.start);\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** Split a statement into words, keeping each one\'s character offsets. */\\r\\nexport function splitWords(statement: string) {\\r\\n  const out: { i: number; text: string; start: number; end: number }[] = [];\\r\\n  const re = /\\\\S+/g;\\r\\n  let m: RegExpExecArray | null;\\r\\n  let i = 0;\\r\\n  while ((m = re.exec(statement)) !== null) {\\r\\n    out.push({ i: i++, text: m[0], start: m.index, end: m.index + m[0].length });\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  return out;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function parseSignal(item: ActivityItem): Signal | null {\\r\\n  const credited = item.credited_answers?.[0];\\r\\n  if (!credited) return null;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const relations: Relation[] = [];\\r\\n  for (const m of credited.matchAll(GROUP)) {\\r\\n    const kind = normalizeKind(m[1]);\\r\\n    const indicators = [...m[2].matchAll(QUOTED)].map((q) => q[1]);\\r\\n    if (!indicators.length) continue;\\r\\n\\r\\n    const anchors = indicators.flatMap((ind) => anchorsFor(ind, item.prompt));\\r\\n    relations.push({ kind, indicators, anchors });\\r\\n  }\\r\\n  if (!relations.length) return null;\\r\\n\\r\\n  const declared = item.explanation?.match(/(\\\\d+)\\\\s+relationship/)?.[1];\\r\\n\\r\\n  return {\\r\\n    order: item.order,\\r\\n    rung: Number(item.category?.match(/Rung\\\\s*(\\\\d)/i)?.[1] ?? 0),\\r\\n    statement: item.prompt,\\r\\n    relations,\\r\\n    declared: declared ? Number(declared) : null,\\r\\n  };\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/* ------------------------------------------------------------------ *\\r\\n * Which way a conditional points\\r\\n * ------------------------------------------------------------------ */\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * A conditional figure is an arrow, and an arrow makes a claim about direction.\\r\\n * Pointing it the wrong way teaches the reversal error, which is the single\\r\\n * mistake the Switchyard exists to drill against, so the direction comes from\\r\\n * an explicit lexicon of standard indicators rather than from position or guess.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * `sufficient` means the indicator\'s own clause is the trigger, and the arrow\\r\\n * leaves it. `necessary` means the indicator\'s clause is what must hold, and\\r\\n * the arrow points into it. Anything not listed gets NO arrowhead: the shaft\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"f320c9","wall_time_seconds":1.0002735,"exit_code":1,"original_token_count":54,"output":"rg: src/features/activity-centre/components/FreeText*: IO error for operation on src/features/activity-centre/components/FreeText*: The filename, directory name, or volume label syntax is incorrect. Type alone is not a complete answer.\\",\\r\\n        \\"A statement can carry more than one relationship. When it does, every relationship must be named with its own indicators, and naming a relationship that is not there is also wrong.\\",\\r\\n        \\"The strongest causal indicator is often a verb rather than a connective. Raises, drives, yields, starves, dries, lowers and homogenizes are causal words, and a student who quotes one has found the right thing.\\",\\r\\n        \\"Requirement language is conditional, not causal. Requires, depends on, contingent on and provided name what must be true; they do not claim that anything made anything happen.\\",\\r\\n        \\"Accept any reasonable quotation of the indicator. Do not penalise capitalisation, a slightly wider or narrower span, or a student who writes only if as only-if.\\",\\r\\n        \\"Comparisons can use a stated second term, a numeric benchmark, or a comparison standard recoverable from context. Do not require two named objects or the word than. Heavier contrasts with the light bell in item 31; longer contrasts with the hard-mortar case in item 36; higher still contrasts with the previous pressure in item 47. Plain adjectives such as deep and adverbs such as efficiently alone are not comparisons.\\",\\r\\n        \\"The explanation count groups relationship types, not every separate claim. Multiple comparisons or causal claims can share one type. Accept any valid indicator for a credited type, including a narrower quoted span, but check that the student pairs it with the right type.\\"\\r\\n      ],\\r\\n      \\"partial_rule\\": \\"Mark \'partial\' when the student names the right type with wrong or missing indicators, quotes the credited indicators but labels the type wrongly, or finds some but not all of the relationships in a multi-relationship statement. Mark \'incorrect\' only when both parts miss.\\",\\r\\n      \\"grading_rubric\\": [\\r\\n        \\"Grade each relationship as a pair: the type and its indicator words together. A correct indicator under the wrong type is a mislabel, not a match.\\",\\r\\n        \\"Correct: every required type in the answer key is present with at least one valid indicator, with no invented type and no contradictory pair.\\",\\r\\n        \\"Partial: the answer contains valid work but misses a type, omits an indicator, gives a type with no indicator, quotes indicators with no type, or pairs an indicator with the wrong type.\\",\\r\\n        \\"Incorrect: neither the relationship types nor the indicators show valid recognition.\\",\\r\\n        \\"Accept equivalent type names (cause, causation, comparison, condition), any capitalisation, any quotation style, and reasonable indicator spans.\\",\\r\\n        \\"A separated comparative such as more ... than or faster ... than needs both halves; one half alone is incomplete.\\",\\r\\n        \\"Comparisons may use another object, a numeric benchmark, or an earlier state clear from context. Do not require two named objects or the word than. Several comparisons can share one comparative type.\\",\\r\\n        \\"On a first unsuccessful attempt, hint at what is missing or mislabelled without quoting the answer key\'s indicators or listing the types.\\"\\r\\n      ]\\r\\n    },\\r\\n    \\"tagline\\": \\"Type plus the exact words. Both halves, every time.\\",\\r\\n    \\"task\\": \\"Tell me what kind of relationship this sentence contains and the exact word or words that create it. If there is more than one, name them all.\\",\\r\\n    \\"rules\\": [\\r\\n      \\"Answer in the game format: type, then the words. Conditional, only if. Causal, caused. Comparative, heavier than.\\",\\r\\n      \\"A conditional says what must be true. A causal says one thing made another happen. A comparative measures one thing against another.\\",\\r\\n      \\"A comparison needs something to compare against: another thing, a number, or an earlier state. That second term can be stated or clear from the sentence. A description like deep or efficient alone isn\'t a comparison.\\",\\r\\n      \\"Causal words are often verbs, not connectives. Raises, drives, dries, starves and lowers are all causal.\\",\\r\\n      \\"Requires, depends on and contingent on are conditional. They name a requirement, not a mechanism.\\",\\r\\n      \\"Some sentences contain all three types. Name each type you find with its own indicators. Several comparisons still count as one type: comparative.\\"\\r\\n    ],\\r\\n    \\"worked_example\\": {\\r\\n      \\"statement\\": \\"A lantern can be lit only if its wick is dry.\\",\\r\\n      \\"credited_answers\\": [\\r\\n        \\"conditional, only if\\"\\r\\n      ],\\r\\n      \\"explanation\\": \\"Only if introduces the necessary condition. A dry wick is required for lighting, which is not the same as saying a dry wick makes the lantern light.  {\\r\\n            \\"type\\": \\"conditional\\",\\r\\n            \\"indicators\\": [\\r\\n              \\"only if\\"\\r\\n            ],\\r\\n            \\"connects\\": \\"the vent opening -> the Nullable author fields remain null. A `cached` reply has `result` and does not debit quota.\\ndocs\\\\implementation-plans\\\\2026-10-01-activity-ai-admission-readiness.md:23:`dispatch_activity_ai_request_v1(p_owner_id uuid, p_request_id uuid, p_claim_id uuid)` returns `dispatched` exactly once for the reserved claim, with the same revalidated canonical `payload`. The Edge Function must observe this acknowledgment before its provider fetch. A repeated dispatch returns `in_flight` or `uncertain` and must not fetch again.\\ndocs\\\\implementation-plans\\\\2026-10-01-activity-ai-admission-readiness.md:25:`finish_activity_ai_request_v1(p_owner_id uuid, p_request_id uuid, p_claim_id uuid, p_result jsonb default null, p_failure_category text default null)` accepts a validated result or one failure category. Result keys are only `verdict`, `feedback`, and optional `covered`. Verdict is `correct`, `partial`, or `incorrect`; feedback is nonblank and at most 2,000 characters. Coverage numbers must belong to the trusted checklist; an empty array is retained. Valid result envelopes are stored exactly, so identical finish retries are replayable.\\ndocs\\\\implementation-plans\\\\2026-10-01-activity-ai-admission-readiness.md:27:Allowed failures are `provider_error`, `timeout`, `malformed_result`, `transport_error`, `cancelled`, `access_revoked`, `source_changed`, and `uncertain`. Root\'s Edge implementation maps gateway quota/credit rejections to `provider_error`, invalid model output to `malformed_result`, and ambiguous network/provider outcomes to `uncertain`. ## assistant

Reviewed `312b62be`. Remaining concrete falsification cases:

- **Instant false credit, reproduced:** item 1 accepts `conditional, only if is not the indicator` and `conditional, only if and because`. Both bypass AI and resolve as correct. The matcher checks word presence without rejecting contradictory or extraneous indicators. [Matcher](/C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-str/src/features/activity-centre/worlds/observatory/observatory.ts:325).
- **Key changes do not invalidate first-attempt eligibility:** complete attempt 1, change only `answer_key`, then request attempt 2. The base SQL compares statement, credited answers, explanation, category and coach, but omits the key. An old judgment can authorize later-attempt disclosure. [SQL](/C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-str/supabase/migrations/20261001120000_activity_ai_admission_v1.sql:85).
- **Prompt-only repair can replay stale judgments:** cache identity hashes source payload, without a grader/model/prompt revision. Unchanged payloads return previously completed results without reaching the repaired prompt. Key/rubric changes invalidate those payloads; prompt changes alone do not. [Cache](/C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-str/supabase/migrations/20261001120000_activity_ai_admission_v1.sql:145).
- **Missing or malformed keys remain admissible:** absent keys pass through; `{"relationships":[]}` passes validation. Test existing assignment snapshots, not just the 48 local JSON keys. Instant matching reads `credited_answers[0]`, while AI also receives `answerKey`, so conflicting snapshots can produce different judgments. [Wrapper](/C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-str/drizzle/migrations/0028_activity_ai_source_answer_key.sql:17).
- **Feedback guarantees remain unenforced:** a syntactically valid first-attempt response that reveals indicators, contradicts its verdict, or gives generic feedback passes parsing and SQL finish. Current payload tests mock the judgment; they prove prompt inclusion, not grading or hint behavior. Final UI displays that feedback directly, and instant credit supplies empty feedback plus the authored explanation. [Parser](/C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-str/supabase/functions/activity-coach/response.ts:4).

Gemini **3.1 Flash Lite** is configured. No edits, deployment, or paid calls. SQL findings are static; live snapshot parity and actual model behavior remain unverified.

Pure, so it runs under Deno in\\n3:// the Edge Function and under Vitest unchanged.\\n4:\\n5:export type RelationshipType = \\"conditional\\" | \\"causal\\" | \\"comparative\\";\\n6:export interface KeyedRelationship {\\n7:  type: RelationshipType;\\n8:  indicators: string[];\\n9:  required_parts?: string[][];\\n10:  connects: string;\\n11:  why: string;\\n12:}\\n13:export interface AnswerKey {\\n14:  relationships: KeyedRelationship[];\\n15:  traps: string[];\\n16:  context_benchmark: string | null;\\n17:}\\n18:\\n19:const TYPES = new Set([\\"conditional\\", \\"causal\\", \\"comparative\\"]);\\n20:const text = (v: unknown, max = 2000) => typeof v === \\"string\\" && v.trim().length > 0 && v.length <= max;\\n21:\\n22:/**\\n23: * Returns the validated key, null when the payload carries no key (legacy\\n24: * activities and historical snapshots), or throws when a key is present but\\n25: * malformed. A malformed upgraded key must never reach the provider.\\n26: */\\n27:export function readAnswerKey(raw: unknown): AnswerKey | null {\\n28:  if (raw === undefined || raw === null) return null;\\n29:  const bad = () => { throw new Error(\\"Malformed answer key\\"); ## assistant

Reviewed `312b62be..d8f82e8a`. Three actionable backend gaps remain:

- **[P2] Leak guard misses explicit disclosures.** Reproduced on item 2 with student response `I am unsure`: `The indicator is if.`, ``The indicator is `if`.`` and `This is an if-then relationship.` all return `hintLeaks=false`. Common indicators require recognized quotation marks, excluding backticks; type detection misses `if-then`. Add these cases to guard tests. [answerKey.ts](/C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-str/supabase/functions/activity-coach/answerKey.ts:62).

- **[P2] SQL validation can fail open through NULL.** A otherwise-valid key with missing `traps` makes the validator return SQL NULL. `if not activity_ai_answer_key_valid_v1(key)` then does not reject it. Edge validation subsequently rejects it, preventing payment, but admission/dispatch and receipt allocation have already occurred. Require `... is not true`; add missing-field tests alongside malformed-field tests. Static finding; PostgreSQL reproduction was unavailable because the referenced PGlite installation is missing. [0029](/C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-str/drizzle/migrations/0029_activity_ai_answer_key_identity.sql:47).

- **[P2] Cache still lacks grader revision.** Identical canonical payloads replay completed judgments before the new validation, prompt, or leak guard runs. An unchanged item can therefore return pre-repair feedback. Key-change tests prove source invalidation, but do not prove invalidation after backend-only repairs. Include a grader revision in identity or explicitly invalidate affected receipts. [grading.ts](/C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-str/supabase/functions/activity-coach/grading.ts:40).

The final source closes the earlier key-only second-attempt gap and revalidates keys at dispatch/finish. Deployment and `enabled=false` remain agent-reported, not independently verified here. No edits, deployment, or paid calls.

CLAUDE.md\\r\\n\\r\\nThis file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Project snapshot\\r\\n\\r\\nLSAT U is a premium ed-tech web app: React 18 + Vite 5 + TypeScript + Tailwind + shadcn/ui, Supabase backend with Edge Functions, hosted on Lovable. The Lovable-connected project is the source of truth; GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db`) is the mirror. Treat the connected project as canonical unless told otherwise.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Second brain (product memory)\\r\\n\\r\\nProduct context, design decisions, pedagogy, and status do NOT live in this repo. They live in the Obsidian vault at `~/ai-os/`. This repo is the code; the vault is the decision layer. Read the vault when starting a session, needing product/design/pedagogy context, or making a decision that touches roadmap or UX. Skip it for trivial code edits.\\r\\n\\r\\nPath sanity check first:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Vault root (the `~/ai-os` directory IS the Obsidian vault): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os`\\r\\n- Code repo root (this project): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/projects/lsatu`\\r\\n\\r\\nIf any older note says `~/Documents/Obsidian Vault/...` or `~/Documents/Claude/02_PROJECTS/LSATU`, treat that as stale — the vault was consolidated into `~/ai-os`. Use the absolute paths above.\\r\\n\\r\\nProtocol is canonical in `~/ai-os/CLAUDE.md` (Session Start / Session End), run via two skills:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **`/pickup`** (start / \\"brief me\\"): reads `wiki/tasks.md` → `wiki/hot.md` → `wiki/dashboard.md` → `wiki/index.md` → latest `wiki/log.md` entry, then gives a 6-8 line briefing (Now / Next / open threads / suggested next) and waits.\\r\\n- **`/wrapup`** (handoff / \\"done for today\\"): proposes which tasks are done (confirm first), strikes them through in `wiki/tasks.md`, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, appends a dated `wiki/log.md` entry, updates the task anchor, gives a 4-line confirmation.\\r\\n\\r\\n`wiki/tasks.md` is the living ledger of Now / Next / Done; finished work is struck through with a date so a new session never re-runs it.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhere LSATU truth lives in the vault (paths relative to `~/ai-os`):\\r\\n\\r\\nThe vault was rebuilt on 2026-07-16. **Live** pages:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Teaching voice (route ALL student-facing copy through it): `wiki/teaching-voice.md`\\r\\n- The tutoring business (offers, channels, priorities): `wiki/entities/aspiring-attorneys.md`\\r\\n- Hubs: `wiki/tasks.md`, `wiki/hot.md`, `wiki/log.md`, `wiki/dashboard.md`, `wiki/index.md`\\r\\n- A student\'s whole record (transcripts, notes, homework): `students/<name>/` — see `students/README.md` for the primary/mirror rule\\r\\n- LSAT teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md` (fills by ingest)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Archived** — the pre-rebuild product knowledge is read-only reference at `archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/`; it is not maintained and may be stale. Re-ingest a page through `raw/` if it becomes load-bearing again rather than editing it in place:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Product context: `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-product.md` (flagship Main Conclusion bootcamp)\\r\\n- Design system (colors, type, role tokens): `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-design.md`\\r\\n- Code/source map: `…/synthesis/lsatu-source-map.md` (regenerable from this codebase)\\r\\n- Quality bar all bootcamps finish to: `…/synthesis/bootcamp-quality-bar.md`\\r\\n- Concepts, entities, domain index: `…/concepts/`, `…/entities/`, `…/index.md`\\r\\n\\r\\nRules: `raw/` is immutable input, never edit it. Read knowledge from `wiki/`, not `raw/`. Use the `obsidian` CLI (`search`, `backlinks`, `read`) for graph traversal (needs the Obsidian app open); flat file reads work without it. When saving deliverables to the vault, use `[[wiki-link]]` syntax and `UPPERCASE-HYPHENATED.md` naming.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Commands\\r\\n\\r\\nScripts (from `package.json`):\\r\\n\\r\\n- `npm run dev` — Vite dev server on port 8080\\r\\n- `npm run build` — production build\\r\\n- `npm run build:dev` — build with development mode flags\\r\\n- `npm run preview` — serve the built bundle\\r\\n- `npm run lint` — ESLint flat config (`eslint.config.js`)\\r\\n- `npm run test` — Vitest single run (runner wired, no specs exist yet)\\r\\n- `npm run test:watch` — Vitest watch mode\\r\\n- Single test file: `npx vitest run path/to/file.test.ts`\\r\\n\\r\\nPackage manager: **npm**. `package-lock.json` is canonical. A stray `bun.lock` / `bun.lockb` exists in the repo — ignore it, do not switch tooling.\\r\\n\\r\\n- `npm run typecheck` — `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.app.json`. Not optional before a push: `vite build` uses esbuild, which strips types without checking them, so a type error ships and auto-deploys. `.claude/safe-push.sh` gates on this.\\r\\n- `npm run test:e2e` / `npm run playwright` — Playwright e2e (`e2e/smoke.spec.ts`).\\r\\n\\r\\nNo Prettier. Formatting is ESLint-only.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Architecture (big picture)\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Routing** — React Router v6. Routes are registered in `src/App.tsx`, pages live in `src/pages/`. `/foyer` is the landing surface. Feature-gated pages are wrapped by `ProtectedRoute`.\\r\\n- **Auth + permissions** — Supabase Auth session plus Lovable Cloud Auth JWT fallback (`@lovable.dev/cloud-auth-js`). Per-feature access flags (`has_practice_access`, `has_drill_access`, `has_chat_access`, `is_admin`, ...) are resolved in `src/hooks/useUserPermissions.ts`. Session death is detected via JWT/401.\\r\\n- **Supabase integration** — client auto-generated at `src/integrations/supabase/client.ts` (Supabase JS v2). DB types are auto-generated — do not hand-edit. Edge Functions (Deno) in `supabase/functions/`: `admin-manage-users`, `analyze-stem-response`, `generate-micro-drill`, `motivation-engine`, `parse-drill-request`, `tutor-chat`, `voice-coach-respond`. SQL migrations in `supabase/migrations/`.\\r\\n- **State** — React Context for cross-cutting concerns (`AuthContext`, `UserSettingsContext`, `QuestionBankContext`, `ThemeContext`, `TimerContext`). TanStack Query v5 for server state.\\r\\n- **Primary navigation — Foyer** — `src/pages/AcademyFoyer.tsx` composes `src/components/foyer/FoyerSidebar.tsx` (state blocks + workspace nav) with `src/components/foyer/FoyerHeroRing.tsx` (3 nodes: Smart Drill, Resume, Ask Joshua). This is the primary nav for the product.\\r\\n- **Messaging / Inbox** — real messaging feature, not cosmetic. Lives in `src/components/inbox/` (`FloatingMessenger`, `ConversationView`, `ThreadList`) plus `src/hooks/useInbox.ts`. One ongoing 1:1 thread per student with the admin (Joshua). Supports PDF attachments. Gated by `has_chat_access`.\\r\\n- **Practice surfaces** — Drill and Wrong Answer Journal (WAJ) are real product surfaces, not launchers. Core logic in `src/lib/`: `adaptiveEngine`, `drillIntelligence`, `achievementEngine`, `gamification`, `questionLoader`, `streakSystem`, `wajService`.\\r\\n- **Admin** — `AdminDashboard` + `admin-manage-users` edge function. Must be desktop AND mobile responsive. Needs granular per-feature toggle control.\\r\\n- **UI primitives** — shadcn/ui (Radix + `class-variance-authority`) in `src/components/ui/`. Icons from `lucide-react` and `@tabler/icons-react`. Framer Motion for animation. Spline for 3D.\\r\\n- **Styling** — Tailwind 3.4 over HSL custom properties in `src/index.css`, surfaced as tokens in `tailwind.config.ts`. `DESIGN.md` is the visual authority; `.impeccable/design.json` is its machine-readable sidecar. Prefer tokens over ad-hoc hex values, and never add a token name whose CSS variable is not defined - three such names (`accent-bronze`, `accent-warm`, `glow-bronze`) shipped broken for five months and silently removed the focus ring from every shared Button.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Conventions\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Git workflow:** Always commit and push directly to `main`. Never create feature branches or pull requests unless explicitly asked. After completing any code change: stage relevant files, commit with a descriptive message, and push to `main`.\\r\\n- **Push via `.claude/safe-push.sh`, not raw `git push`.** Lovable auto-commits to `main` mid-session; a raw push can get rejected or race a concurrent commit. The script fetches, merges origin/main if it diverged (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes.\\r\\n- **Never roll `main` back.** Do not use Lovable\'s \\"Revert to commit\\", \\"revert and resend\\", or history restore, and never reset or force-push `main`. On 2026-09-27 an accidental \\"revert and resend\\" (b998596) silently undid three merged PRs across 121 question-bank files, and students saw the wrong questions again. To undo your own work, `git revert <your SHA>` only. If a Lovable dialog offers a revert, dismiss it; if one fires, stop and tell Joshua before repairing anything.\\r\\n- Path alias `@/` → `./src/` (set in `vite.config.ts` and `tsconfig.json`).\\r\\n- TypeScript strict mode is **off** (`strictNullChecks: false`, `noImplicitAny: false`, `noUnusedLocals: false`). Do not flip these without explicit approval — it would cascade into a very large diff.\\r\\n- Lovable-specific plumbing that must not be removed:\\r\\n  - `lovable-tagger` Vite plugin injects `data-lov-id` in dev\\r\\n  - `vite-plugin-pwa` configured with start URL `/foyer`\\r\\n  - Lovable preview Service Worker guard in `src/main.tsx`\\r\\n- Identity rules: email sign-in, profile identity uses **name** (not username), instructor is displayed as **\\"Joshua\\"**, students display their real profile names.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Cross-tool instruction hygiene\\r\\n\\r\\n`CLAUDE.md` is the fuller project source of truth. `AGENTS.md` is the Codex-facing adapter and should stay aligned without becoming a second full copy.\\r\\n\\r\\n- When updating project instructions, update both files as needed: put durable detail here, and keep `AGENTS.md` as a concise adapter.\\r\\n- When creating a Claude skill that Codex should use, also copy it to `.agents/skills/` with its `SKILL.md` and supporting files.\\r\\n- When creating a Claude agent that Codex should spawn, also create a matching `.codex/agents/<name>.toml` and place the converted instructions in `developer_instructions`.\\r\\n- When adding shared knowledge, put it in `docs/`, `references/`, or `templates/` so every tool can read the same source.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Verification (must not skip)\\r\\n\\r\\nAny change to `src/components/` or `src/pages/` is not \\"done\\" until it has been viewed running in the app (dev server + browser tool, e.g. Playwright or chrome devtools MCP), with a screenshot or DOM check as evidence. Do not report a UI change complete on the strength of the diff alone. This has been the single biggest recurring failure across past sessions (repeated \\"it\'s not working\\" / \\"i dont see it\\" corrections) — treat it as a hard gate, not a suggestion.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Responsive fixes carry a wider gate: verify across the full emulate matrix BEFORE pushing, not after Joshua reports it.** That means narrow, mid, and wide viewports plus the sidecar-docked case, every time. A responsive defect that gets pushed as \\"fixed\\" and does not hold costs a second full session — this has already happened (a pushed Home-page fix that did not survive, then 13 screenshots and 8 emulate cycles to actually close it). One matrix pass before the push is cheaper than either.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Product rules (must not regress)\\r\\n\\r\\n- Foyer (`FoyerSidebar` + `FoyerHeroRing`) is the primary navigation. The Foyer additionally hosts a `GooeyDock` quick-launch row well below the orbital ring; this dock is allowed only on `/foyer`. Do not add docks elsewhere.\\r\\n"}'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 8.3 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"1dcbcf","wall_time_seconds":0.6843822,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":89,"output":"{\\r\\n  \\"id\\": \\"bd36e5c7-567c-43e4-b785-dcb706d1024f\\",\\r\\n  \\"prevId\\": \\"0bd38c17-2f5c-42fb-a80a-3c43dea6bce7\\",\\r\\n  \\"version\\": \\"7\\",\\r\\n  \\"dialect\\": \\"postgresql\\",\\r\\n  \\"tables\\": {},\\r\\n  \\"enums\\": {},\\r\\n  \\"schemas\\": {},\\r\\n  \\"views\\": {},\\r\\n  \\"sequences\\": {},\\r\\n  \\"roles\\": {},\\r\\n  \\"policies\\": {},\\r\\n  \\"_meta\\": {\\r\\n    \\"columns\\": {},\\r\\n    \\"schemas\\": {},\\r\\n    \\"tables\\": {}\\r\\n  }\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"bb11bd","wall_time_seconds":0.7070082,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1539,"output":"---\\r\\ntitle: Teaching Voice\\r\\ntype: concept\\r\\ntags: [lsat-u, voice, pedagogy, content]\\r\\ncreated: 2026-06-10\\r\\nupdated: 2026-10-02\\r\\nstatus: active\\r\\nsubject: Teaching Voice\\r\\naliases: []\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Teaching Voice\\r\\n\\r\\nHow LSAT U sounds, across every surface. This is the canonical voice doc: route all student-facing and brand copy through it. Built from a grill with Joshua (2026-06-13), then tested and enriched against the full corpus of his real correspondence (944 messages he authored, mid-2025 to mid-2026, from the Gmail takeout; 2026-07-18). Claims below marked with a quote are evidenced by that corpus.\\r\\n\\r\\nIt replaces the old two-register / 15-named-tools \\"preserve verbatim\\" profile (deleted 2026-06-10) and folds in the brand/marketing register the earlier draft was missing.\\r\\n\\r\\n**LSAT teaching standard, selected 2026-10-02:** Joshua chose the supplied Conditional Reasoning transcription as the reference for how LSAT material should sound and unfold. Read [LSAT teaching voice standard](../references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md) before drafting lessons, notes, explanations, drills, flashcards, quizzes, or instructional feedback. It contains the detailed source analysis, original calibration examples, progression, and review rubric. [Source identity and limits](sources/conditional-reasoning-voice.md) are preserved separately. This choice develops the teaching-content register below; correspondence and marketing retain their own registers.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Core voice (every register)\\r\\n\\r\\nThe constant under all three registers below. This is Joshua on the page.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Quiet authority.** State the value and let it land. Never oversell, never beg, never perform enthusiasm.\\r\\n- **Specific, never generic.** Real scores, the actual skill, the student\'s own history. Praise is a specific observation, not \\"great job.\\"\\r\\n- **Honest about the work, gentle about the person.** Be blunt that the LSAT is hard (\\"expect this to be exhausting and very challenging\\"). Never be blunt about a student\'s shortcomings. Honesty points at the material; warmth points at the person. When someone slips, nudge gently, never accuse.\\r\\n- **Light, grounded metaphor, understated in email.** In his real correspondence the recurring image is quiet: \\"partner with you on your LSAT journey,\\" \\"getting set up,\\" \\"foundational.\\" The vivid images (runway, climb, march toward November, an easy door to walk through) belong to authored teaching and brand copy, not to email. Keep metaphor light and grounded; never float off into abstraction.\\r\\n- **Balanced rhythm.** Air between ideas. Real correspondence is short and single-purpose (median ~280 characters). In teaching, vary sentence and paragraph length with the reasoning: give an explanation room, then let a short sentence settle the consequence. Do not impose email brevity on a lesson or cut its intermediate reasoning.\\r\\n- **Plain professional diction.** No corporate jargon, no startup hype (\\"unlock,\\" \\"crush,\\" \\"level up,\\" \\"game-changer\\"; \\"crush\\" appears once in 944 messages). Contractions are the norm, not the exception. Exclamation points are genuine, not performed: a warm message often carries one, occasionally more, especially in openers (\\"Sorry for such a long delay!\\"). He is not exclamation-averse.\\r\\n- **Forward-looking.** End on the next step or what\'s ahead.\\r\\n- **No em dashes, ever.** Use periods, colons before lists, the middot (·) as a separator, and numbered steps for procedures.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Openings, closings, and signature phrases\\r\\n\\r\\nThe mechanical fingerprint of his email, from the real corpus. Match these when drafting as Joshua.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Greeting.** \\"Hi [Name],\\" is the default (used ~519x). \\"Hey [Name],\\" for established/returning students (~229x, warmer). \\"Hello [Name],\\" (~105x) and \\"Dear [Name],\\" (~18x, formal) for first contact or a more formal note.\\r\\n- **Sign-off.** \\"Best,\\" and \\"Best regards,\\" are the workhorses (~360x / ~237x). \\"Thanks,\\" when it\'s a favor or logistics (~146x). \\"Warmly,\\" / \\"Warm regards,\\" (~54x) for a genuinely warm note. \\"Talk soon,\\" (~9x) for someone he\'ll see again soon.\\r\\n- **Signature.** Almost always signed **\\"Joshua\\"** (844 of 944 messages). The name is near-universal; include it.\\r\\n- **Signature phrases (his verbal fingerprint, use naturally, don\'t overuse):** \\"let me know\\" (by far the most common, ~390x), \\"looking forward,\\" \\"I hope\\" / \\"hope all is well\\" / \\"hope you,\\" \\"no worries at all,\\" \\"happy to\\" / \\"more than happy to,\\" \\"just wanted to make sure,\\" \\"make sure,\\" \\"no rush,\\" \\"of course.\\"\\r\\n- **Punctuation he actually uses:** semicolons (correctly, ~41x); ellipses essentially never. (For em-dashes, the hard rule in Core voice governs: never.)\\r\\n\\r\\n## The apology / accountability move\\r\\n\\r\\nOne of his most frequent and characteristic behaviors, and worth naming on its own. When he is late to reply, or missed or mishandled something, he opens by owning it plainly, often with a brief honest reason, then pivots straight to the fix. No over-apologizing, no excuses dressed up.\\r\\n\\r\\n> I\'m so sorry. I thought I had let you know ... I clearly didn\'t communicate that as well as I should have.\\r\\n\\r\\n> I\'ve been away from work for personal reasons, but I\'m back now and getting caught up.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe pattern: own it in the first line, give the real reason in one clause, move to what happens next. Warm, candid, brief.\\r\\n\\r\\n## When something on his side broke (approved shape, 2026-09-11)\\r\\n\\r\\nFrom the Sandra booking-error reply, edited by Joshua line by line over three drafts and then sent as-is. Use this shape whenever a student hit a bug or a mistake that was his. The sent text is the reference:\\r\\n\\r\\n> Hi Sandra,\\r\\n>\\r\\n> I\'m sorry about this. The error was on my end, not yours.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> To confirm: none of your bookings went through, so nothing is on the calendar yet. The page was showing a few times that the system then refused, and it looks like you landed on those. That is fixed now.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Your link is the same one as before:\\r\\n> [link]\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Pick any time it shows and it will book. If it\'s easier, just send me the time you were trying for and I\'ll add it to the schedule myself.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> If you run into any other issues, let me know.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Once again, my apologies.\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"c1a56b","wall_time_seconds":0.6967184,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":305,"output":"207:          slug: string\\r\\n223:          slug: string\\r\\n388:      activity_assignments: {\\r\\n390:          activity_id: string | null\\r\\n490:          stimulus_slug: string\\r\\n502:          stimulus_slug: string\\r\\n1122:          slug: string\\r\\n1142:          slug: string\\r\\n1174:          slug: string\\r\\n1181:          slug: string\\r\\n1348:          booking_slug: string | null\\r\\n1407:          event_slug: string\\r\\n1415:          event_slug: string\\r\\n1879:          slug: string\\r\\n1889:          slug: string\\r\\n2492:          slug: string\\r\\n2516:          slug: string\\r\\n3072:          slug: string\\r\\n3087:          slug: string\\r\\n3329:          slug: string\\r\\n3349:          slug: string\\r\\n4358:          slug: string\\r\\n4373:          slug: string\\r\\n4749:          slug: string\\r\\n4760:          slug: string\\r\\n5118:          event_slug: string\\r\\n6593:          activity_id: string\\r\\n6600:          activity_id: string\\r\\n6989:          event_slug: string\\r\\n7212:          booking_slug: string | null\\r\\n7268:          event_slug: string\\r\\n7383:        Args: { _event_slug: string }\\r\\n7387:        Args: { _email: string; _event_slug: string }\\r\\n7410:        Args: { _action: string; _ip_hash: string; _slug: string }\\r\\n7438:          event_slug: string\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"e73e1c","wall_time_seconds":2.1044463,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1699,"output":"// Domain types for the Activity Centre. These describe the shape of the jsonb\\r\\n// columns (instructions, items) and the rows of the three Activity tables.\\r\\n// They intentionally do not import the generated Supabase Row types for the\\r\\n// jsonb internals, since Postgres types those as `Json`; the client casts the\\r\\n// validated shape to these on read.\\r\\n\\r\\nexport type Verdict = \\"correct\\" | \\"partial\\" | \\"incorrect\\";\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * Who decided an attempt\'s verdict.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * `fast-path` is a world\'s deterministic local verdict, settled with no model\\r\\n * call. It covers exact authored matches and formal parsers such as the\\r\\n * Switchyard. `coach` is the edge function. `fallback` is a\\r\\n * Coach outage, where the credited answer is revealed and no verdict is earned.\\r\\n * Recorded so the share of attempts that never reach the model is a measured\\r\\n * number rather than a claim.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport type JudgeSource = \\"fast-path\\" | \\"coach\\" | \\"fallback\\";\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * What the answer column is showing about the last submission.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * \\"unchecked\\" is not a verdict and must never be scored as one. It is the state\\r\\n * an item is left in when the Coach could not be reached: the student answered,\\r\\n * nobody judged it, and stamping a red \\"Not yet\\" over it was a claim the app\\r\\n * had no basis for.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport type FeedbackState = Verdict | \\"unchecked\\";\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * `abandoned` is a Read, Recall, Colour section she started again from the\\r\\n * library without finishing. Its rows are kept and it is never graded; it is\\r\\n * not `completed`, because completed with rows is exactly what gets graded.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport type ActivityStatus = \\"assigned\\" | \\"in_progress\\" | \\"completed\\" | \\"abandoned\\";\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * How a student answers an Activity\'s items.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * `spoken-recall` is Read, Recall, Colour: she speaks rather than types, and\\r\\n * the unit she answers is a chunk of an item rather than the item itself. It is\\r\\n * listed here because the column is a plain string and every other format is,\\r\\n * not because the runner branches on it; the world does that.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport type ItemFormat = \\"free-text\\" | \\"mcq\\" | \\"variation\\" | \\"spoken-recall\\";\\r\\n\\r\\n/** One authored prompt within an Activity. */\\r\\nexport interface ActivityItem {\\r\\n  order: number;\\r\\n  /** The statement the student must respond to. */\\r\\n  prompt: string;\\r\\n  /** Author-supplied accepted answer(s); ground truth for the Coach. */\\r\\n  credited_answers: string[];\\r\\n  /** Why the credited answer is correct; revealed after the attempt cap. */\\r\\n  explanation: string;\\r\\n  /** Optional grouping label (e.g. \\"Universal Statement\\"). */\\r\\n  category?: string;\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * Structured grading key (Spot the Relationship). Server-side grading input\\r\\n   * only; never shown to the student. The explanation stays the student copy.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  answer_key?: {\\r\\n    relationships: { type: string; indicators: string[]; required_parts?: string[][]; connects: string; why: string }[];\\r\\n    traps: string[];\\r\\n    context_benchmark: string | null;\\r\\n  };\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * One conceptual unit of a chunk, and the only thing completeness is measured\\r\\n * against.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Written in plain analytic language, never in the stimulus\'s own wording. A\\r\\n * recall is REQUIRED to be in the student\'s own words, so a detail phrased as a\\r\\n * quotation could not be matched without failing the own-words criterion at the\\r\\n * same moment.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport interface ChunkDetail {\\r\\n  /** Authored position in the list, 1-based. */\\r\\n  n: number;\\r\\n  text: string;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * One step of a spoken-recall item: what the student reads, and what has to\\r\\n * survive when she says it back with the text hidden.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * Chunking rule, which is settled and not a per-item judgement call: default to\\r\\n * one sentence, allow smaller down to a comma or a third of a sentence, and\\r\\n * never combine sentences, because the first sentence\'s details are what get\\r\\n * lost when you do. Round down, not up. The one exception is a chunk that is\\r\\n * not yet a complete thought, which may take slightly more.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport interface RecallChunk {\\r\\n  n: number;\\r\\n  /** Verbatim from the item\'s prompt. The chunks must reassemble it exactly. */\\r\\n  text: string;\\r\\n  details: ChunkDetail[];\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * A Read, Recall, Colour item: one whole stimulus, cut into chunks.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * `prompt` is the entire stimulus and is never shown whole to the student; she\\r\\n * only ever sees one chunk at a time, and only until she taps past it. It is\\r\\n * stored whole because the chunks are checked against it and because the final\\r\\n * cumulative round is scored against everything in it.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * `credited_answers` holds one entry: a model retelling of the whole stimulus\\r\\n * in plain words with every detail intact. It is the reference for that final\\r\\n * cumulative round, and the clearest single statement of what \\"close enough\\"\\r\\n * means here. Per-chunk there is no credited answer, only `details`.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport interface RecallItem extends ActivityItem {\\r\\n  chunks: RecallChunk[];\\r\\n  /**\\r\\n   * The chunk numbers after which the student says back everything so far with\\r\\n   * nothing on screen. The load grows every round, which is the point of the\\r\\n   * drill and the part software can enforce that paper cannot.\\r\\n   */\\r\\n  cumulative_after: number[];\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * How a spoken-recall Activity is run.\\r\\n *\\r\\n * `stimulus` is v1: one stimulus at a time, with cumulative rounds. `section`\\r\\n * is the captured drill, where the unit of work is a whole LR section run\\r\\n * unbroken against a clock, with no cumulative round and no coach in the loop.\\r\\n * Absent means `stimulus`, so every Activity authored before section mode keeps\\r\\n * behaving exactly as it did.\\r\\n */\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * `section-library` is the door: an Activity whose content is the list of\\r\\n * sections a student picks from, rather than a section itself. It carries no\\r\\n * `source_section` and no clock, which is why the database exempts it from the\\r\\n * completeness check that a real section must satisfy.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport type ActivityMode = \\"stimulus\\" | \\"section\\" | \\"section-library\\";\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * The Read\'s type, as captured: Loophole, Implication, Contradiction, Rephrase.\\r\\n * Stored as the single captured letter rather than a spelled-out name, because\\r\\n * the key is ground truth and re-spelling it invites drift.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport type TheReadType = \\"L\\" | \\"I\\" | \\"C\\" | \\"R\\";\\r\\n\\r\\n/**\\r\\n * The minutes a section is meant to take, per accommodation. Time is a scored\\r\\n * criterion, so the goal cannot be one number: a student with 53% or 70% extra\\r\\n * time is not slow when she takes longer, and grading her against the standard\\r\\n * goal would score her accommodation as a failure.\\r\\n */\\r\\nexport interface TimeGoalMinutes {\\r\\n  standard: number;\\r\\n  accommodation_53: number;\\r\\n  accommodation_70: number;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n/** One captured LR question that hangs off a stimulus. */\\r\\nexport interface SectionQuestion {\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 20.5 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"5212cb","wall_time_seconds":10.0141061,"session_id":22413,"original_token_count":0,"output":""}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"faa364","wall_time_seconds":0.5900902,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":1323,"output":"---\\r\\ntype: synthesis\\r\\nsubject: LSAT teaching voice standard\\r\\ncreated: 2026-10-02\\r\\nupdated: 2026-10-03\\r\\naliases: [LSAT voice reference, Conditional transcription writing standard]\\r\\ntags: [lsat-u, voice, pedagogy, writing]\\r\\nsources:\\r\\n  - ../wiki/sources/conditional-reasoning-voice.md\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# LSAT teaching: make the reasoning sound like someone thinking\\r\\n\\r\\n**This is Joshua\'s selected reference for writing LSAT teaching material.** Use it for bootcamp lessons, lecture notes, explanations, guided examples, drills, flashcards, quizzes, reference pages, and instructional feedback. It develops the teaching-content register in [the canonical teaching voice](../wiki/teaching-voice.md). That document still governs correspondence and marketing in their own registers.\\r\\n\\r\\nJoshua selected the supplied **Conditional Reasoning** transcription on 2026-10-02 because of its writing, tone, word choice, cadence, point of view, progression, and depth. The goal is to reproduce those qualities closely through original wording and examples. The central quality is patient, conversational reasoning that the reader can follow and eventually perform.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe source is a calibration example. This guide separates **observed features** from **instructions for our material**. A source habit is not automatically a rule, a research finding, or permission to import a topic. Matching the voice does not require repeating the same metaphors, anecdotes, exercise sentences, or personal claims.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Find the part you need\\r\\n\\r\\n- [The evidence and coverage](#the-evidence-and-coverage)\\r\\n- [The voice in one original passage](#the-voice-in-one-original-passage)\\r\\n- [How the teacher relates to the reader](#how-the-teacher-relates-to-the-reader)\\r\\n- [Sentence length and spoken cadence](#sentence-length-and-spoken-cadence)\\r\\n- [Word choice and useful verbal habits](#word-choice-and-useful-verbal-habits)\\r\\n- [Questions that make the reader think](#questions-that-make-the-reader-think)\\r\\n- [Transitions, repetition, metaphor, and emphasis](#transitions-explain-why-the-next-move-follows)\\r\\n- [The complete source progression and depth](#the-complete-source-progression-and-depth)\\r\\n- [What to carry into the Conditional Bootcamp](#what-to-carry-into-the-conditional-bootcamp)\\r\\n- [Accuracy and method boundaries](#accuracy-and-method-boundaries)\\r\\n- [Calibration examples and review](#calibration-examples-for-different-lsat-surfaces)\\r\\n- [A reusable writing brief](#a-reusable-writing-brief)\\r\\n\\r\\n## The evidence and coverage\\r\\n\\r\\nThe [source summary](../wiki/sources/conditional-reasoning-voice.md) identifies the [preserved HTML](../raw/sources/conditional-reasoning-voice-standard/conditional_reasoning_transcription.html), [provenance](../raw/sources/conditional-reasoning-voice-standard/PROVENANCE.md), and SHA-256. It ends with six basic drill items and an answer-key reference. One line is explicitly marked partially obscured. The answer key is not supplied. This analysis covers the entire attachment; it cannot reconstruct the missing line or establish the contents of the larger chapter.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe HTML says its formatting was adjusted. No author is identified in the file. The exercise wording attributed to Shakespeare is separate from the narrator\'s teaching voice.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The voice in one original passage\\r\\n\\r\\nThe following is a new calibration passage. Its example was created for this guide; it is not a source quotation or an approved bootcamp page.\\r\\n\\r\\n> A conditional tells us that if one thing is true, something else has to be true too.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Suppose a shop has this rule: **Every parcel with a blue sticker has been inspected.**\\r\\n>\\r\\n> You pick up a parcel with a blue sticker. What do you know about it?\\r\\n>\\r\\n> It has been inspected. The rule covers every parcel with that sticker, including the one in your hand. A blue-sticker parcel that hadn\'t been inspected would break the rule.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> Now turn the parcel over. It has an inspection stamp, but you can\'t see a blue sticker. Does it have to have one?\\r\\n>\\r\\n> We don\'t know. The rule tells us something about parcels with blue stickers. It doesn\'t tell us that every inspected parcel gets a sticker.\\r\\n>\\r\\n> That\'s the distinction we need: **what the sentence makes certain, and what it leaves open.**\\r\\n\\r\\nNotice the opening definition before the example, the small prediction, the explanation connecting the rule to the case, and the change in what the reader is given. The last sentence names a distinction the reader has just seen. The passage has a conversational rhythm because it follows a person\'s thoughts. Casual vocabulary alone would not accomplish that.\\r\\n\\r\\nIn an interactive lesson, pause after the first question and again after the inspection-stamp question. Reveal each explanation after the learner has had an opportunity to answer. The printed passage shows the whole progression for calibration.\\r\\n\\r\\n## How the teacher relates to the reader\\r\\n"}'}]

text(await tools.apply_patch("*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-str-backend/docs/implementation-plans/2026-10-13-relationship-backend-draft.md\n+# Relationship grading backend draft\n+\n+Local draft based on d8f82e8a. No managed writes, enablement, deployment or model calls.\n+\n+Migration 0030 follows applied 0029 without modifying it. Missing key fields and malformed JSON array shapes return false before admission allocates a receipt. The canonical payload gains a trusted `graderRevision` from the private configuration row. Advance that revision for future prompt, model or guard changes. Cache, dispatch, finish and second-attempt validation all revalidate it. Old request IDs intentionally return `request_conflict` rather than silently buying a fresh judgment; the frontend must offer a fresh first-attempt identity after a contract revision. A revision change also requires a newly confirmed first attempt before attempt 2 can disclose answers.\n+\n+`allowed_activity_ids` is an optional private allowlist. NULL preserves existing scope; an empty array denies all sources. For a future Spot-only activation, independently verify the Spot the Relationship activity IDs, then restrict the array to those IDs. Assignments use their trusted `activity_id`; previews use their own activity ID. Unlinked assignments fail closed under restricted scope. The gate also blocks cache replay, dispatch and finish after scope removal. This migration does not set the array or change `enabled`, budgets, roles or grants on admission RPCs.\n+\n+The hint guard recognizes backtick quotations, explicit unquoted indicator disclosures and the if-then type alias. Ordinary uses of common words remain permitted. The replacement hint makes no claim that an incorrect response contains valid work. This remains a lexical guard, not a guarantee against every paraphrased answer disclosure or model grading error.\n+\n+Local checks: focused mock-provider Vitest tests; isolated PGlite admission/key identity scripts; typecheck; production build. PGlite uses throwaway in-memory fixtures and fixture-only configuration changes. No live backend acceptance is claimed.\n+\n+Review the migration number against current main before integration. Reviewers should coordinate the frontend request-conflict recovery and any future activation/deployment separately.\n*** End Patch"));

feedback: string; covered?: number[] };\\r\\n\\r\\n/** No judgment is returned unless the model supplies both a verdict and feedback. */\\r\\nexport function parseCoachResponse(data: unknown, ideas: { n: number }[] | null): CoachModelResult {\\r\\n  const envelope = data as { choices?: { message?: { content?: unknown } }[] } | null;\\r\\n  const raw = envelope?.choices?.[0]?.message?.content;\\r\\n  const parsed = typeof raw === \\"string\\" ? ## assistant

I found a safer recovery path: keep a completed first attempt valid when the trusted question, key, and rubric are unchanged. For a terminal receipt changed only by the grader revision, the server can explicitly permit one persisted identity replacement and retry. In-flight or uncertain receipts won’t get that permission. This avoids restarting the student’s attempt flow or requiring a separate frontend fix.

text(await tools.apply_patch("*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-str-backend/src/features/activity-centre/hooks/activityCoachRequest.ts\n@@\n export function retainCoachRequest(actor: string, source: CoachSource, itemOrder: number, studentResponse: string, attemptNo: number, storage: Storage = localStorage): CoachRequest {\n@@\n }\n+\n+/** Called only after the server confirms a terminal, revision-only conflict.\n+ * Ordinary conflicts and uncertain outcomes must keep their original ID.\n+ * If another caller already replaced it, reuse that durable replacement.\n+ */\n+export function replaceRevisionCoachRequest(actor: string, request: CoachRequest, storage: Storage = localStorage): CoachRequest {\n+  const current = retainCoachRequest(actor, request, request.itemOrder, request.studentResponse, request.attemptNo, storage);\n+  if (current.requestId !== request.requestId) return current;\n+  // Keep the storage identity identical to retainCoachRequest (no requestId or\n+  // contractVersion in the source), so reload/retry finds this replacement.\n+  const identity = { actor, sourceKind: request.sourceKind, sourceId: request.sourceId, itemOrder: request.itemOrder, studentResponse: request.studentResponse, attemptNo: request.attemptNo };\n+  const key = `lsatu:activity-coach:v1:${JSON.stringify(identity)}`;\n+  const requestId = crypto.randomUUID();\n+  const saved = JSON.stringify({ identity, requestId });\n+  storage.setItem(key, saved);\n+  if (storage.getItem(key) !== saved) throw new Error(\"The replacement grading request could not be saved.\");\n+  return { ...request, requestId };\n+}\n*** End Patch"));
